# List of Formula One driver records

This page is accurate as of the [2026 Bahrain Grand Prix](80201648.md). Drivers who have competed in the [2026 Formula One World Championship](71681684.md) are highlighted in **bold**.

For other records relating to Formula One, see [List of Formula One records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Formula_One_records_\(disambiguation\)).

The [World Championship of Drivers](10854.md) has been held since [1950](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1950_Formula_One_season). Driver records listed here include all rounds which formed part of the World Championship since 1950: this includes the [Indianapolis 500](159018.md) from 1950–1960 (although it was not run to [Formula One](10854.md) rules), and the 1952 and 1953 World Championship Grands Prix (which were run to [Formula Two](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_Two) rules). Formula One races that were not qualification rounds for the World Championship are not included, and [sprints](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sprint_\(Formula_One\)) are only included when specified.

## Races entered and started

Drivers are considered to be entered into a race if they attempt to compete in at least one official practice session with the intent of participating in the race. These drivers are noted on the entry list for that race. A driver is considered to have started a race if they line up on the grid or at the pit lane exit for the start of the race. If a race is stopped and restarted, participation in any portion of the race is counted only if that portion was in any way counted towards the final classification (e.g. races stopped before the end of the leader's second lap were declared null and void prior to 2005).

Notes

- [Juan Manuel Fangio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Manuel_Fangio) qualified on pole position four times at the [Monaco Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monaco_Grand_Prix) between 1950–1957 when it was held as a championship race; the [1952 edition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1952_Monaco_Grand_Prix) was non-championship sportscar race whilst the race was not held in 1951 and 1953–1954.

## Points

See also: [List of Formula One World Championship points scoring systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Formula_One_World_Championship_points_scoring_systems)

Throughout the history of the World Championship, the points-scoring positions and the number of points awarded to each position have varied, along with the number of events per season in which points could be scored.

## Sprints

Sprint races were introduced in [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Formula_One_World_Championship) as an addition to select races; since 2022, [points have been awarded to the top eight finishers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Formula_One_points_systems#Sprint_races).

| Most driving penalties in a season | 10 | [Venezuela](32374.md) [Pastor Maldonado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pastor_Maldonado) ([2014](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Formula_One_World_Championship)) | [^cite_note-James_2013-29][^cite_note-PlanetF1_2023-68] |

| Shortest distance driven in career | 2 m | [Germany](11867.md) [Ernst Loof](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ernst_Loof) ([1953 German Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1953_German_Grand_Prix))[^cite_note-269] | [^cite_note-270][^cite_note-James_2013-29] |

| Most hat-tricks in a season | 6 | [Netherlands](21148.md) **[Max Verstappen](41758713.md)** ([2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Formula_One_World_Championship)) | [^cite_note-Hardy_2023-82] |

[^cite_note-Indy-5]: &#x20;The [Indianapolis 500](159018.md) was a round of the World Drivers' Championship 11 times (from 1950 to 1960). Drivers competing in the Indianapolis 500 during these years were credited with participation in a World Championship race and the top five finishers were credited with World Championship points.

[^cite_note-BWT_Mercedes-8]: &#x20;Including [BWT Mercedes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racing_Point_F1_Team).

[^cite_note-Fangiostarts-11]: &#x20;Fangio entered 52 separate races and started 51 of them, but in 7 Grands Prix (1950 Italian, 1951 French, 1953 Belgian, 1953 Swiss, 1956 Argentine, 1956 Monaco and 1956 Italian) shared 5 cars (all mentioned but 1950 Italian and 1953 Belgian) with other drivers which would reduce his percentages to 41.38%.

[^cite_note-Ascaristarts-12]: &#x20;Ascari entered 33 separate races and started 32 of them, but in 3 Grands Prix (1950 Italian, 1951 French and 1953 German) shared 3 drives with 1 additional car (in latter with Luigi Villoresi) which would reduce his percentages to 37.14%.

[^cite_note-17]: &#x20;Some sources extend Ascari's sequence to 9 wins, including the subsequent [1953 Dutch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1953_Dutch_Grand_Prix) and [Belgian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1953_Belgian_Grand_Prix) Grands Prix and discounting the intervening [1953 Indianapolis 500](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1953_Indianapolis_500), on the basis that very few of the European drivers competed in the Indianapolis 500 when it was part of the Drivers' Championship.

[^cite_note-Fangio-20]: &#x20;Fangio began racing in Grands Prix in 1948 — before the first World Championship.

[^cite_note-Farina-21]: &#x20;Farina began racing in Grands Prix in the 1930s — before the first World Championship.

[^cite_note-26]: &#x20;Shared drive with Stirling Moss

[^cite_note-Alonso2008SPbackgrid-30]: &#x20;A year after the victory, Renault were charged with [race fixing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renault_Formula_One_crash_controversy), which led to the team's suspended disqualification; however, the original results were left in place.

[^cite_note-Farina_double_podium-61]: &#x20;Farina's total of 20 podiums includes both 2nd and 3rd places at the [1955 Argentine Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1955_Argentine_Grand_Prix).

[^cite_note-sprint_points_incl.-79]: &#x20;Including sprint points.

[^cite_note-80]: &#x20;See talk page for supporting calculation. *[Autosport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autosport)* listed it as 998.

[^cite_note-88]: &#x20;Some sources give him 50 starts, others give him 51 starts.

[^cite_note-94]: &#x20;Some sources attribute the record at 148 due to some conjecture over whether De Cesaris had 147 or 148 retirements; De Cesaris ran out of fuel and pushed his car over the finish line at the [1987 Belgian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1987_Belgian_Grand_Prix).

[^cite_note-Partial_seasons-116]: &#x20;This includes partial seasons.

[^cite_note-Alonso2002season-117]: &#x20;Alonso did not compete in the 2002 World Championship as he spent the season as Renault's test driver, so he won the World Championship in the fourth season he contested.

[^cite_note-Andretti1973season-119]: &#x20;Andretti did not compete in the 1973 World Championship, so he won the World Championship in the tenth season he contested.

[^cite_note-159]: &#x20;All of Hulme's eight career wins were not from pole position.

[^cite_note-178]: &#x20;Hulme won the 1967 World Championship without ever having scored a pole, although six years later, in 1973, Hulme racked up the solitary pole of his 112-race career.

[^cite_note-182]: &#x20;The gap to third place in this qualifying was also 0.000s and was set by [Heinz-Harald Frentzen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heinz-Harald_Frentzen).

[^cite_note-228]: &#x20;Some sources listed De Cesaris's sequence to 22 retirements, starting from the [1986 Australian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1986_Australian_Grand_Prix) to the [1988 Canadian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988_Canadian_Grand_Prix) and including the [1987 Belgian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1987_Belgian_Grand_Prix), [1987 Australian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1987_Australian_Grand_Prix), and [1988 Canadian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988_Canadian_Grand_Prix) Grands Prix on the basis that he did not complete the races.

[^cite_note-233]: &#x20;Verstappen won the 2023 World Championship at the [Qatar sprint race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Qatar_Grand_Prix#Sprint), before racing at the Grand Prix. Sprint races do not count to the tally of this record.

[^cite_note-247]: &#x20;Every race in the 2012 season had the aforementioned six drivers compete.

[^cite_note-260]: &#x20;Button was given a drive-through penalty for speeding behind the safety car.

[^cite_note-265]: &#x20;Button received a total of 70 places (three ten-place and eight five-place) of grid penalties for exceeding the allowed allocation of five of his power unit components, unscheduled engine and gearbox changes.

[^cite_note-267]: &#x20;Vettel was recorded to have sped in pit lane during his first free practice session as Formula One driver.

[^cite_note-269]: &#x20;Loof started one World Championship race at the 1953 German Grand Prix but only made it two metres off the starting grid. The 1952 and 1953 World Championships were run to Formula Two regulations; however, *World Championship* and *Formula One* are commonly referred to synonymously and are treated as such for these records. [Marco Apicella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marco_Apicella), who raced a mere 800 metres (2,600 ft) at the [1993 Italian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1993_Italian_Grand_Prix), is frequently referred to as having the shortest Formula One career but that is a technicality.
